has order type less than

\omega^{\omega^{\omega(\omega(\NR(x)+\gamma+4)a_{k}+1)}}=a_{k+1}

Since the equivalences classes of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}\left(\underset{s\in E_{1,k}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp s\right)/{\asymp}
are finite, the
ones of
E_{1,k+1}/{\asymp}
are also finite. Finally, using Lemmas
6.2.2
and
6.2.2
,

\NR(t)

\ \leq\

(\omega\oplus\omega\otimes\gamma\oplus\omega)+\underset{j=0}{\overset{k+1}{\sum}}\underset{i=i_{j}}{\overset{k_{P_{j}}-1}{\sum}}\NR\left(\ln|P_{j}(i)|\right)+\underset{j=0}{\overset{k+1}{\sum}}\max(0,k_{P_{j}}-i_{j})

\ \leq\

\omega(\NR(x)+\gamma+4)

Then,
\NR\left(\underset{t^{\prime}\in E_{1,k+1}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp t^{\prime}\right)\leq\omega(\NR(x)+\gamma+4)a_{k+1}

We conclude thanks to the induction principle.

(vi)

By easy induction, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
a_{k}<\lambda
.

(vii)

Using (iv), we get that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\underset{k=0}{\overset{N}{\bigcup}}E_{1,2k}
is an initial segment of

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,2k}
. We also have that
\underset{k=0}{\overset{N}{\bigcup}}E_{1,2k+1}
is an initial segment of

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,2k+1}
. Using (v), we get that
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,2k}
has order type at most

\sup\left\{\left.\underset{k=0}{\overset{N}{\oplus}}a_{2k}\ \vphantom{N\in\mathbb{N}}\right|\ N\in\mathbb{N}\right\}=\sup\left\{\left.a_{2N}\ \vphantom{N\in\mathbb{N}}\right|\ N\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\underset{\text{by (vi)}}{\leq}\lambda

Similarly,
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,2k+1}
has order type at most
\lambda
. Using Proposition
2
, we conclude that
E_{1}
has order type at most
2\lambda
.

Using again proposition
2
, point (vii) above and the properties of
E_{2}
and
E_{3}
mentioned in the beginning of this proof, we get that
E
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
2\lambda+\omega(\gamma+1)
.
∎

Corollary 7.1
.

Let
x=\underset{i<\nu}{\overset{}{\sum}}r_{i}\omega^{a_{i}}
such that

\exists u=\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}\quad\exists r\in\mathbb{R}\quad\forall a\in\supp x\quad\exists\eta\prec\ln u\qquad\omega^{a}=\partial u\exp(r\ln u+\eta)

Let
\gamma
be the smallest ordinal such that
\kappa_{-\gamma}\prec^{K}P(k_{P})
for all path
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\eta)
.
Let
\lambda
the least
\varepsilon
-number greater than
\NR(x)
and
\gamma
. Then
\underset{\ell=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{\ell}(x)
is reverse well-ordered with order type less at most
\omega^{\omega\left(2\lambda+\omega(\gamma+1)+1\right)}

Proof.

Just use Propositions
7.1.1
,
7.1.1
and
2
.
∎

7.1.2
Case
\varepsilon\succ\ln u

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
x
be a surreal number. Let
P
be the dominant path of
x
and
Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(x)
. Then,
P(k_{P})\succeq^{K}Q(k_{Q})
. In particular, for all ordinal
\beta
, if
\kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})
, then
\kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}Q(k_{Q})
.

Proof.

(i)

We first claim that for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
P(i)\succeq Q(i)
. We prove it by induction.

•

For
i=0
,
P(0)
is the leading term of
x
and
Q(0)
is some term of
x
. Therefore,
P(0)\succeq Q(0)
.

•

Assume
P(i)\succeq Q(i)
.
P(i+1)
is the leading term of
\ln|P(i)|
.
P(i)
and
Q(i)
are both infinitely large. Then
\ln|P(i)|
and
\ln|Q(i)|
are both positive infinitely large. If
Q(i+1)\succ P(i+1)
then, in particular,
\ln|Q(i)|\succ\ln|P(i)|
what is impossible since
P(i)\succeq Q(i)
. Then
P(i+1)\succeq Q(i+1)
.

We conclude thanks to induction principle.

(ii)

Take
k=\max(k_{P},k_{Q})
. Using (i), we have :

P(k_{P})\asymp^{K}P(k)\succeq Q(k)\asymp^{K}Q(k_{Q})

Hence,
P(k_{P})\succeq^{K}Q(k_{Q})
.

∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Assume
x=\omega^{a}=\partial u\exp\varepsilon
with
\varepsilon\succ\ln u
and
u=\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}
.
Let
b\in\supp\Phi(\omega^{a})
. Then, we have one of theses cases :

•

there is a path
P\in\mathcal{P}(\eta)
and
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\omega^{b}\asymp\partial u\exp\left(\varepsilon-\hskip-20.00003pt\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{j=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln\left|\mathchoice{\dfrac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}{\dfrac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}{\frac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}{\frac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}\right|\right)

and
\forall j\in\left\llbracket\,0\ ;\ i-1\,\right\rrbracket\qquad P(j)=P_{0}(j)

•

There is some
(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)
such that there is some
\eta\prec\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}
such that

\omega^{b}\asymp\partial(\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp\eta

where
\eta=\varepsilon+\eta^{\prime}
and
\eta^{\prime}
only depends on
\alpha,\beta,n,m
and
P_{0}
, the dominant path of
\varepsilon
:

\eta^{\prime}=\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)>_{lex}(\beta,m)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\hskip-10.00002pt\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\zeta,p)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-10.00002pt\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|

or
\eta^{\prime}=\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)\geq_{lex}(\alpha,n)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-10.00002pt\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|

Proof.

We have
\Phi(\omega^{a})=\left(1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}{\dfrac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}{\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}{\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}\right)\omega^{a}-\partial\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial u}{s}}{\dfrac{\partial u}{s}}{\frac{\partial u}{s}}{\frac{\partial u}{s}}\right)\exp\varepsilon

Let
b\in\supp\Phi(\omega^{a})
. Then either

b\in\supp\left(\left(1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}{\dfrac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}{\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}{\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}\right)\omega^{a}\right)

or
b\in\supp\left(\partial\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial u}{s}}{\dfrac{\partial u}{s}}{\frac{\partial u}{s}}{\frac{\partial u}{s}}\right)\exp\varepsilon\right)

•

First case
:
b\in\supp\left(\left(1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}{\dfrac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}{\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}{\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{s}}\right)\omega^{a}\right)
. Then there is a path
P
, which is not the dominant path, such that

\omega^{b}\asymp\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial P}{s}}{\dfrac{\partial P}{s}}{\frac{\partial P}{s}}{\frac{\partial P}{s}}\omega^{a}\asymp\mathchoice{\dfrac{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}{\dfrac{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}{\frac{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}{\frac{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}\omega^{a}

where
P_{0}
is the dominant path of
\varepsilon
. Using Lemma
7.2
, we get

\omega^{b}\asymp\omega^{a}\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln\left|\mathchoice{\dfrac{P(i)}{P_{0}(i)}}{\dfrac{P(i)}{P_{0}(i)}}{\frac{P(i)}{P_{0}(i)}}{\frac{P(i)}{P_{0}(i)}}\right|\right)

•

Second case
:
b\in\supp\left(\partial\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial u}{s}}{\dfrac{\partial u}{s}}{\frac{\partial u}{s}}{\frac{\partial u}{s}}\right)\exp\varepsilon\right)
. First notice that
\partial\partial u=S_{u}\partial u
where

S_{u}=-\underset{\beta<\alpha\ m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)-\underset{m=1}{\overset{n-1}{\sum}}\exp\left(-\underset{\beta<\alpha\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\alpha}\right)

Hence, if
b\in\supp\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial\partial u}{s}}{\dfrac{\partial\partial u}{s}}{\frac{\partial\partial u}{s}}{\frac{\partial\partial u}{s}}\exp\varepsilon\right)
, there is some
(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)
such that

\omega^{b}\asymp\omega^{a}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}{\dfrac{\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}{\frac{\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}{\frac{\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}

Therefor,
\omega^{b}\asymp\omega^{a}\exp\left(\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)\geq_{lex}(\beta,m)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)

Notice that
\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)\geq_{lex}(\beta,m)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}\sim\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}\succ P_{0}(0)

and then

\omega^{a}

\ \asymp\

\partial(\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp\left(\varepsilon+\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)>_{lex}(\beta,m)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}\right.

\left.\qquad\vphantom{\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)>_{lex}(\beta,m)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}}-\underset{(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\zeta,p)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)

Since
\varepsilon-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\sim\varepsilon\prec\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}

and
\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)>_{lex}(\beta,m)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\zeta,p)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}\prec\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}

Moreover,

\NR\left(\ln\partial(\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta})+\varepsilon-\underset{(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\zeta,p)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}\right)\leq\NR(x)

and using Proposition
6.4
,

\NR\left(\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)>_{lex}(\beta,m)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)

\ \leq\

\NR(\partial P_{0})

\ \leq\

k_{P_{0}}(\NR(x)+1)+\omega(\gamma+1)

We then conclude that there is some
\eta\prec\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}
such that

\omega^{b}=\partial(\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp\eta

and by Corollary
6.2.2
,

\NR(\omega^{b})\leq(k_{P_{0}}+1)(\NR(x)+1)+\omega(\gamma+1)

Now assume
b\in\supp\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial s}{s^{2}}}{\dfrac{\partial s}{s^{2}}}{\frac{\partial s}{s^{2}}}{\frac{\partial s}{s^{2}}}\omega^{a}\right)
.
Notice that

\partial s

\ =\

s\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\partial\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)

\ =\

s\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)\right.

\left.\vphantom{\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ \kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}}\qquad+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\partial\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)

We then have the following sub-cases :

➢

There is some
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and some ordinal
\beta
such that
\kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})
such that

\omega^{b}

\ \asymp\

\mathchoice{\dfrac{\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}{\dfrac{\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}{\frac{\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}{\frac{\exp\left(-\underset{\zeta<\beta\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{p=1}{\overset{m-1}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)}}\omega^{a}

\ \asymp\

\partial(\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp\left(\varepsilon+\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)\geq_{lex}(\alpha,n)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right)

with

\varepsilon-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\zeta\geq\alpha\ p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\sim\varepsilon\prec\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}

We then conclude that there is some
\eta\prec\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}
such that

\omega^{b}=\partial(\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp\eta

and by Corollary
6.2.2
,

\NR(\omega^{b})\leq(k_{P_{0}}+1)(\NR(x)+1)+\omega(\gamma+1)

➢

There is some path
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
and some
i\geq 1
such that for all
j<i
,
P(j)=P_{0}(j)
and

\omega^{b}\asymp\mathchoice{\dfrac{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{j=i}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(j)|\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{j=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(j)|\right)}}{\dfrac{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{j=i}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(j)|\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{j=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(j)|\right)}}{\frac{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{j=i}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(j)|\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{j=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(j)|\right)}}{\frac{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{j=i}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(j)|\right)}{\exp\left(-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}+\underset{j=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(j)|\right)}}\omega^{a}

As in the first case, we get

\omega^{b}\asymp\omega^{a}\exp\left(-\hskip-10.00002pt\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\beta\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\beta\ |\ P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-30.00005pt\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{j=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln\left|\mathchoice{\dfrac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}{\dfrac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}{\frac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}{\frac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}\right|\right)

∎

Proposition 7.4
.

Assume
x=\omega^{a}=\partial u\exp\varepsilon
with
\varepsilon\succ\ln u
and
u=\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}
.
Let
P_{0}
be the dominant path of
\varepsilon
.
We denote for
P_{1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
,
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
(\beta,m)\leq_{lex}(\alpha,n)
,

e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)

\ =\

-k\underset{i=0}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|+\underset{j=1}{\overset{k}{\sum}}\underset{i=i_{j}}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\ln|P_{j}(i)|

-\underset{j=1}{\overset{k}{\sum}}\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\geq\alpha,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P_{j}(k_{P_{j}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}

-k^{\prime}\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}{\kappa_{-\beta}}+\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{k+k^{\prime}}{\sum}}\underset{i=i_{j}}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{j}(i)|

-\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{k+k^{\prime}}{\sum}}\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P_{j}(k_{P_{j}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}\par

We now define
E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}
as follows:

e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)\in E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}

\ \Leftrightarrow\

P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)\setminus\{P_{0}\}

\wedge\quad P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)

\wedge\quad i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}

\wedge\quad i_{k+1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}

\wedge\quad\forall j\in\left\llbracket\,1\ ;\ k+k^{\prime}\,\right\rrbracket\ \exists j^{\prime}\in\left\llbracket\,0\ ;\ j-1\,\right\rrbracket

\qquad\qquad\forall i\in\left\llbracket\,0\ ;\ i_{j}-1\,\right\rrbracket\quad P_{j^{\prime}}(i)=P_{j}(i)

\wedge\quad\forall j\in\left\llbracket\,k+1\ ;\ k+k^{\prime}\,\right\rrbracket

\qquad\qquad\supp P_{j}(i_{j})\subseteq\supp e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{j}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{j}\end{matrix}\right)

E_{1}^{(\beta,m)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}\underset{k\in\mathbb{N},\ k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}&(\beta,m)\neq(\alpha,n)\\
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,0}^{(\beta,m)}&(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)\end{array}\right.

Define sets
E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}
as follows:

•

If
(\beta,m)\neq(\alpha,n)
, then

-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\hskip-10.00002pt\underset{\gamma^{\prime}<\gamma,\ \ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-10.00002pt\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma^{\prime}}-\underset{\ell=1}{\overset{p}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}\in E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}

iff
\gamma>\beta
,
p\in\mathbb{N}
and there is some
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
such that
\kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})

•

If
(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)
, then

-\underset{j=0}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(j)|-\underset{\gamma>\zeta>\alpha,\ \ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{\ell=1}{\overset{p}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}\in E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}

iff
\gamma>\alpha
,
p\in\mathbb{N}
and there is some
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
such that
\kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})
.

Let also

E_{3}^{(\beta,m)}

\ =\

\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}\left\{\left.-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{p}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}{\kappa_{-\beta}}\ \vphantom{p\geq m+2}\right|\ p\geq m+2\right\}&(\beta,m)\neq(\alpha,n)\\
\varnothing&(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)\end{array}\right.

E^{(\beta,m)}

\ =\

E_{1}^{(\beta,m)}\cup E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}\cup E_{3}^{(\beta,m)}

and
\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
be the monoid it generates. Finally, let
H^{(\beta,m)}
defined by cases as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}\left\{\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)>_{lex}(\beta,m)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}\hskip 80.00012pt\right.\\
-\underset{(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\zeta,p)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|,\\
\left.\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)\geq_{lex}(\alpha,n)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\right\}&(\beta,m)\neq(\alpha,n)\\
\{0\}&(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)\end{array}\right.

Let
b\in\underset{q=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{q}(\omega^{a})
. Then, there are
\eta\in H^{(\beta,m)}
and
y\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
such that

\omega^{b}\asymp\partial(\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp(\varepsilon+\eta+y)

Proof.

We prove it by induction on
q
.

•

If
b\in\supp\Phi^{0}(\omega^{a})
, then
b=a
and
y=0
with
(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)
and
\eta=0
works.

•

Assume the property for some
q\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
b\in\supp\Phi^{q+1}(\omega^{b})
. Then there is

c\in\supp\Phi^{q}(\omega^{a})
such that
b\in\supp\Phi(\omega^{c})
. Apply the induction hypothesis on
c
. Take
(\beta,m)
,
\eta\in H^{(\beta,m)}
and
y\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
such that

\omega^{c}\asymp\partial(\ln_{m}\beta)\exp(\varepsilon+\eta+y)

➢

If
(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)
, then
y,\varepsilon\prec\ln_{n+1}\kappa_{-\beta}
. Hence, using Lemma
7.1.1
, we get that there is
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon+\eta+y)
such that

\omega^{b}

\ \asymp\

\partial(\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp\left(\varepsilon+\eta+y-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}\vphantom{\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}}\right.

\left.\qquad-\hskip-20.00003pt\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\right)

If
P(0)
a term of
\varepsilon
, up to some real factor, then there is a real number
s
and some
e\in E_{1,0,1}^{(\beta,m)}
such that

s\exp e

\ =\

\exp\left(-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\hskip-20.00003pt\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\right)

Then
y+e\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
and
\omega^{b}\asymp\partial(\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp(\varepsilon+y+e)
. If not, then
P(0)
is a term
of
\eta+y
. Hence, we have the following cases:

\because

P(0)=s\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\beta}
for some
s\in\mathbb{R}^{*}_{-}
and
p\geq m+2
. Then,

-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|

\ =\

\ln|s|-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{p}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}\in\ln|s|+E_{3}^{(\beta,m)}

Then,

y-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\hskip-20.00003pt\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\in\mathbb{R}+\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle

\because

P(0)=s\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\gamma}
with
\gamma>\beta
and
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that there is some path
Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
such that
\kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}Q(k_{Q})
. Then

-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\hskip-20.00003pt\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\in\ln|s|+E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}

Then,

y-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|\in\mathbb{R}+\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle

\because

There are some paths
P_{1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
and some integers
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}
such that

e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)\in E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}

and
\exists y^{\prime}\in\left\langle E\right\rangle\qquad y=y^{\prime}+e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)

and finally such that
P(0)\in\mathbb{R}z
for some
z
a term of some
\ln|P_{j}({i_{k+k^{\prime}+1}}^{\prime})|
with
j\in\left\llbracket\,0\ ;\ k+k^{\prime}\,\right\rrbracket
and
{i_{k+k^{\prime}+1}}^{\prime}\geq i_{j}
.
Let
P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}
be the following path :

P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}(i)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}P_{j}(i)&i\leq{i_{k+1}}^{\prime}\\
z&i={i_{k+1}}^{\prime}+1\\
P(i-{i_{k+1}}^{\prime}-1)&i>{i_{k+1}}^{\prime}+1\end{array}\right.

Then,
P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}\in\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon)
. Moreover,

\partial P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}=\underbrace{P_{j}(0)\cdots P_{j}({i_{k+1}}^{\prime})}_{\neq 0}\underbrace{\partial P}_{\neq 0}

Then
P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
. Note also that for all
\beta
,

\kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}(k_{P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}})\Longleftrightarrow\kappa_{-\beta}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})

Finally,

-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|

\ =\

-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}(k_{P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}

\qquad\qquad+\underset{i={i_{k+1}}^{\prime}+1}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}(i)|+\ln\underbrace{\left|\mathchoice{\dfrac{P(0)}{z}}{\dfrac{P(0)}{z}}{\frac{P(0)}{z}}{\frac{P(0)}{z}}\right|}_{\in\mathbb{R}^{*}_{+}}

From that we derive that

y-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P(i)|

\ =\

y^{\prime}+e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}+1}\end{matrix}\right)+\ln\left|\mathchoice{\dfrac{P(0)}{z}}{\dfrac{P(0)}{z}}{\frac{P(0)}{z}}{\frac{P(0)}{z}}\right|\in\mathbb{R}+\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle

where
i_{k+k^{\prime}+1}={i_{k+k^{\prime}+1}}^{\prime}+1
and
P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}(i_{k+k^{\prime}})=z
has indeed its support (which is reduced to a singleton) included
in the one of
e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+k^{\prime}},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)
.

Then there is a real number
s
, and
e\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
such that

\omega^{b}\asymp\partial(\ln_{m}\beta)\exp(\varepsilon+\eta+e+s)\asymp\partial(\ln_{m}\beta)\exp(\varepsilon+\eta+e)

Then we get the property at rank
q+1
.

➢

If
(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)
, we have
\eta=0
and write

y=y^{\prime}+e^{(\alpha,n)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
\varnothing\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)

with,
y^{\prime}\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
and,
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Using Lemma
7.3
, we have

\because

First case
:
\omega^{b}\asymp\partial u\exp(\varepsilon+y+e)

where

e=-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\geq\alpha,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{j=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln\left|\mathchoice{\dfrac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}{\dfrac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}{\frac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}{\frac{P(i+j)}{P_{0}(j)}}\right|

for some path
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon+y)
and some
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\forall j\in\left\llbracket\,0\ ;\ i-1\,\right\rrbracket\qquad P(j)=P_{0}(j)

Indeed,
y\in\left\langle E^{(\alpha,n)}\right\rangle
. In particular,
y\prec\varepsilon
and then
\varepsilon+y\sim\varepsilon
so that
P_{0}
is also the dominant path of
\varepsilon+y
.

·

If
P(0)
is, up to a real factor, a term of
\varepsilon
,
then we get that there is some path
Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
and a real number
s
such that

y+e=y^{\prime}+e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k},Q\\
\varnothing\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k},i\end{matrix}\right)+s

Since
y\prec\varepsilon
, and
P\neq P_{0}
, we also have
Q\neq P_{0}
. Then
y+e\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle+E_{1,k+1,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}+s
. Let

y^{\prime\prime}=y+e-s\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle

then,
\omega^{b}\asymp\partial u\exp(\varepsilon+y^{\prime\prime})

In particular,
y^{\prime\prime}\prec\varepsilon
.

·

If
P(0)
is a term of
y
, and more precisely if it can be written as
P(0)=s\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\gamma}
for
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
\gamma\geq\alpha
such that

P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}Q(k_{Q})

for some path
Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)\setminus\{P_{0}\}
. Then,

e=-\underset{j=0}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(j)|-\underset{\gamma>\zeta>\alpha,\ \ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{\ell=1}{\overset{p+i}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\mathds{1}_{i=0}\ln|s|\in E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}+\mathbb{R}

Then
y+e-\ln|s|\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
and since
e\prec\varepsilon
,
y+e-s\prec\varepsilon
and

\omega^{b}\asymp\partial u\exp(\varepsilon+y+e-\ln|s|)

·

If
P(0)
is a term of
y
, and more precisely if it can be written as
P(0)=s\ln|P_{\ell}(j)|
for some
s\in\mathbb{R}
and some
\ell\in\left\llbracket\,0\ ;\ k+k^{\prime}\,\right\rrbracket
(actually it is true if we have chosen well the
y^{\prime}
in the beginning, but up to a renaming, it is true). Consider the following path

Q(p)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}P_{\ell}(p)&p\leq j\\
P(p-j)&p>j\end{array}\right.

We have
Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
and

y+e=y^{\prime}+e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{1},\dots,P_{k},Q\\
\varnothing\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k},j\end{matrix}\right)+\ln|s|

Then
y+e-\ln|s|\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
and since
e\prec\varepsilon
,
y+e-s\prec\varepsilon
and

\omega^{b}\asymp\partial u\exp(\varepsilon+y+e-\ln|s|)

This concludes the first case.

\because

Second case
: There are
(\beta^{\prime},m^{\prime})<_{lex}(\alpha,n)
and
\eta^{\prime}\in H^{(\beta,m)}
such that
\omega^{b}\asymp\partial(\ln_{m^{\prime}}\kappa_{-\beta^{\prime}})\exp(\varepsilon+\eta^{\prime}+y)
. This immediately conclude the second case.

We then have the property at rank
q+1
.

Thanks to the induction principle, we conclude that the property holds for any
q\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

Corollary 7.0
.

Let
x
be a surreal number such that

\exists u=\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}\quad\exists r_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{*}\quad\exists a_{0}\in\textnormal{No}\quad\forall a\in\supp x\quad\exists\varepsilon\sim r\omega^{a_{0}}\succ\ln u\qquad\omega^{a}\asymp\partial u\exp\varepsilon

Let
\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)=\left\{\left.P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(x)\ \vphantom{\begin{array}[]{c}P(1)=r\omega^{a_{0}}\\
\forall i\geq 1\quad P(i+1)\sim\ln|P(i)\end{array}}\right|\ \begin{array}[]{c}P(1)=r\omega^{a_{0}}\\
\forall i\geq 1\quad P(i+1)\sim\ln|P(i)\end{array}\right\}

It is the set of all the possible dominant paths of the epsilon to which we add the corresponding term of
x
at the beginning.
We denote for
P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)
,
P_{1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(x)
,
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
(\beta,m)\leq_{lex}(\alpha,n)
,

e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{0};P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)

\ =\

-k\underset{i=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|-k^{\prime}\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}{\kappa_{-\beta}}

-\underset{j=1}{\overset{k}{\sum}}\hskip-10.00002pt\underset{\scriptsize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\geq\alpha,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P_{j}(k_{P_{j}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-30.00005pt\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}

+\underset{j=1}{\overset{k}{\sum}}\underset{i=i_{j}}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\ln|P_{j}(i)|

-\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{k+k^{\prime}}{\sum}}\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P_{j}(k_{P_{j}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}

+\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{k+k^{\prime}}{\sum}}\underset{i=i_{j}}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{j}(i)|

We now define
E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}
as follows:

e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{0};P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)\in E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}

\ \Leftrightarrow\

P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)\wedge P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)\setminus\{P_{0}\}

\wedge\quad P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(x)

\wedge\quad i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}

\wedge\quad i_{k+1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0,1\}

\wedge\quad\forall j\in\left\llbracket\,1\ ;\ k+k^{\prime}\,\right\rrbracket\ \exists j^{\prime}\in\left\llbracket\,0\ ;\ j-1\,\right\rrbracket

\qquad\qquad\forall i\in\left\llbracket\,0\ ;\ i_{j}-1\,\right\rrbracket\quad P_{j^{\prime}}(i)=P_{j}(i)

\wedge\quad\forall j\in\left\llbracket\,k+1\ ;\ k+k^{\prime}\,\right\rrbracket

\qquad\qquad\supp P_{j}(i_{j})\subseteq\supp e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{0};P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{j}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{j}\end{matrix}\right)

E_{1}^{(\beta,m)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}\underset{k\in\mathbb{N},\ k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}&(\beta,m)\neq(\alpha,n)\\
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,0}^{(\beta,m)}&(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)\end{array}\right.

Define sets
E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}
as follows:

•

If
(\beta,m)\neq(\alpha,n)
, then

-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\beta}-\hskip-10.00002pt\underset{\gamma^{\prime}<\gamma,\ \ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-10.00002pt\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma^{\prime}}-\underset{\ell=1}{\overset{p}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}\in E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}

iff

\gamma>\beta
,
p\in\mathbb{N}
and there is some
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
such that
\kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})

•

If
(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)
, then

-\underset{j=0}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(j)|-\underset{\gamma>\zeta>\alpha,\ \ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{\ell=1}{\overset{p}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}\kappa_{-\gamma}\in E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}

iff

\gamma>\alpha
,
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)
,
P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\gamma}
and there is some
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(\varepsilon)
such that
\kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P(k_{P})
.

Let also

E_{3}^{(\beta,m)}

\ =\

\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}\left\{\left.-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{p}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}{\kappa_{-\beta}}\ \vphantom{p\geq m+2}\right|\ p\geq m+2\right\}&(\beta,m)\neq(\alpha,n)\\
\varnothing&(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)\end{array}\right.

E^{(\beta,m)}

\ =\

E_{1}^{(\beta,m)}\cup E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}\cup E_{3}^{(\beta,m)}

and
\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
be the monoid it generates. Finally, let

H^{(\beta,m)}
defined by cases as follows:

•

If
(\beta,m)\neq(\alpha,n)
, then

H^{(\beta,m)}

\ =\

\left\{\left.\begin{array}[]{r}\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)>_{lex}(\beta,m)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\zeta,p)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}\\
-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\end{array}\ \vphantom{P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)}\right|\ P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)\right\}

\qquad\bigcup\left\{\left.\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}(\zeta,p)\geq_{lex}(\alpha,n)\\
\zeta\ |\ \kappa_{-\zeta}\succeq^{K}P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-20.00003pt\ln_{p}\kappa_{-\zeta}-\underset{i=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|\ \vphantom{P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)}\right|\ P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)\right\}

•

If
(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)
, then

H^{(\beta,m)}=\left\{\left.-\ln|P_{0}(x)|\ \vphantom{P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)}\right|\ P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)\right\}

Let
b\in\underset{q=0}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{q}(x)
. Then, there are
\eta\in H^{(\beta,m)}
and
y\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
such that

\omega^{b}\asymp\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial u}}{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial u}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial u}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial u}}\exp(\eta+y)

Proof.

Since
\Phi
is strongly linear, we just need to apply Proposition
7.4
to each term of
x
. Each path of
\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)
involved is shifted one rank. In
H^{(\beta,m)}
we the add
\ln|P_{0}(0)|
compare to Proposition
7.4
. Then
\exp(\eta)
gives also
|\partial u\exp\varepsilon|
. We just remove it so that it does not appear twice.
∎

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
x
be a surreal number such that there
u
of the form
u=\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}
,
r\in\mathbb{R}^{*}
and
a_{0}\in\textnormal{No}
such that
r\omega^{a_{0}}\succ\ln u
and

\forall a\in\supp x\quad\exists\varepsilon\sim r\omega^{a_{0}}\qquad\omega^{a}\asymp\partial u\exp\varepsilon

Let
\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)=\left\{\left.P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(x)\ \vphantom{\begin{array}[]{c}P(1)=r\omega^{a_{0}}\\
\forall i\geq 1\quad P(i+1)\sim\ln|P(i)\end{array}}\right|\ \begin{array}[]{c}P(1)=r\omega^{a_{0}}\\
\forall i\geq 1\quad P(i+1)\sim\ln|P(i)\end{array}\right\}

Consider
E_{1}^{(\beta,m)}
,
E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}

and
E_{3}^{(\beta,m)}
as defined in Corollary
7.1.2
.
Let
\xi
be the smallest ordinal such that
\kappa_{-\xi}\prec^{K}P(k_{P})
for all path
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(x)
.
Let
\lambda
the least
\varepsilon
-number greater than
\NR(x)
and
\xi
. Then
E^{(\beta,m)}=E_{1}^{(\beta,m)}\cup E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}\cup E_{3}^{(\beta,m)}
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
2\lambda+\omega(\xi+1)
.

Proof.

First notice that
E_{3}^{(\beta,m)}
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
\omega
.
E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}
is also reverse well-ordered with order at most
\omega\otimes\xi
. We then focus on
E_{1}^{(\beta,m)}
. For the moment, we will assume
(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)
.

(i)

We first claim that for all
i\geq 3
and all path
P\in\mathcal{P}(x)
,
P(i)\prec P(2)\preceq\ln_{m+2}\kappa_{-\beta}
. It is indeed the same proof as the point (i) of the proof of Proposition
7.1.1
.

(ii)

We claim that for all path
P\in\mathcal{P}(x)
, if
P(2)\asymp\ln_{m+2}\kappa_{-\beta}
, then, if
r
is the real number such that

P(2)\sim r\ln_{m+2}\kappa_{-\beta}
, we have
0<r\leq 1
. It is indeed the same proof as the point (ii) of the proof of Proposition
7.1.1
.

(iii)

For all
j
and
i\geq 2
,
\ln|P_{j}(i)|\preceq\ln_{m+3}\kappa_{-\beta}\prec\ln_{m+2}\kappa_{-\beta}
. Indeed, using (i), we know that
P_{j}(i)\preceq\ln_{m+2}\kappa_{-\beta}
. Then, there is a natural number
m\geq 1
such that
|P_{j}(i)|\leq m\ln_{m+2}\kappa_{-\beta}
. Using the fact that
\ln
is increasing,

\ln|P_{j}(i)|\leq\ln_{m+3}\kappa_{-\beta}+\ln m\preceq\ln_{m+3}\kappa_{-\beta}\prec\ln_{m+2}\kappa_{-\beta}

(iv)

We now claim that
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}>\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}+2}^{(\beta,m)}
. Indeed, using (ii) and (iii) if
e_{1}\in\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}
, then there is
s\in\mathchoice{\left[\,-(k+1)\,;-k\,\right]}{\left[\,-(k+1)\,;-k\,\right]}{\left[\,-(k+1)\,;\,-k\,\right]}{\left[\,-(k+1)\,;\,-k\,\right]}
such that

e_{1}\sim s\ln_{m+2}\kappa_{-\beta}
. Similarly, for
e_{2}\in\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}+2}^{(\beta,m)}
, there is
s^{\prime}\in\mathchoice{\left[\,-(k+3)\,;-(k+2)\,\right]}{\left[\,-(k+3)\,;-(k+2)\,\right]}{\left[\,-(k+3)\,;\,-(k+2)\,\right]}{\left[\,-(k+3)\,;\,-(k+2)\,\right]}
such that
e_{2}\sim s^{\prime}\ln_{m+2}\kappa_{-\beta}
.

(v)

We define the following sequence :

•

a_{0}=1

•

a_{k+1}=\omega^{\omega^{\omega(\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+1)a_{k}+1)}}

We show that
E_{1,k,0}^{(\beta,m)}
is reverse well-ordered with order type less than
a_{k}
. We also claim that the equivalence classes of
E_{1,k,0}^{(\beta,m)}/{\asymp}
are finite and that

\NR\left(\underset{t\in E_{1,k,0}^{(\beta,m)}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp t\right)\leq\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+1)a_{k}

We show it by induction on
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

•

For
k=0
,
E_{1,0,0}^{(\beta,m)}=\{0\}
. Then it is reverse well-ordered with order type
1
. We also have

\NR\left(\underset{t\in E_{1,0,0}^{(\beta,m)}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp t\right)=\NR(1)=1\leq\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+1)

•

Assume the property for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
t\in E_{1,k+1,0}^{(\beta,m)}
. Let
(P_{0},0),(P_{1},i_{1}),\dots,(P_{k+1},i_{k+1})
minimal for the order
(<_{lex},<)_{lex}
such that

t=e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{0};P_{1},\dots,P_{k+1}\\
\varnothing\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+1}\end{matrix}\right)

Then,

t

\ =\

e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{0};P_{1}\dots,P_{k}\\
\varnothing\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\end{matrix}\right)-\underset{i=1}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{0}(i)|

\qquad-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma\geq\alpha,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ P_{0}(k_{P_{0}})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P_{k+1}(k_{P_{k+1}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-50.00008pt\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\gamma}+\underset{i=i_{k+1}}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{k+1}(i)|

Write
s=e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{0};P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
\varnothing\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\end{matrix}\right)
and consider the following path:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}R(0)=\exp s\\
R(i)=P_{k+1}(i-1+i_{k+1})&i>0\end{array}\right.

It is indeed a path since, by definition of
E_{1,k+1,0}^{(\beta,m)}
,
\supp P_{k+1}(i_{k+1})
must be contained in
\supp s
. We then have,

\exp t=\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial R}{\partial P_{0}[1:]}}{\dfrac{\partial R}{\partial P_{0}[1:]}}{\frac{\partial R}{\partial P_{0}[1:]}}{\frac{\partial R}{\partial P_{0}[1:]}}

Moreover,
R\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}\left(\underset{s\in E_{1,k,0}^{(\beta,m)}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp s\right)
. By assumption on
x
, the set
\left\{\left.P_{0}[1:]\ \vphantom{P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)}\right|\ P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)\right\}
is a singleton. Therefore, so is the set
\left\{\left.\partial P_{0}[1:]\ \vphantom{P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)}\right|\ P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(x)\right\}
. By induction hypothesis and Proposition
1
,
E_{1,k+1,0}^{(\beta,m)}
has order type less than

\omega^{\omega^{\omega(\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+1)a_{k}+1)}}=a_{k+1}

Since the equivalences classes of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}\left(\underset{s\in E_{1,k,0}^{(\beta,m)}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp s\right)/{\asymp}
are finite, the
ones of
E_{1,k+1,0}^{(\beta,m)}/{\asymp}
are also finite. Finally, using Lemmas
6.2.2
and
6.2.2
,

\NR(t)

\ \leq\

(\omega\otimes\xi)+\underset{i=1}{\overset{k_{P_{0}}-1}{\sum}}\NR(\ln|P_{0}(i)|)+k_{P_{0}}

\qquad+\underset{j=1}{\overset{k+1}{\sum}}\underset{i=i_{j}}{\overset{k_{P_{j}}-1}{\sum}}\NR\left(\ln|P_{j}(i)|\right)+\underset{j=0}{\overset{k+1}{\sum}}\max(0,k_{P_{j}}-i_{j})+4

\ \leq\

\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+1)

Then,
\NR\left(\underset{t^{\prime}\in E_{1,k+1}^{(\beta,m)}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp t^{\prime}\right)\leq\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+1)a_{k+1}

We conclude thanks to the induction principle.

(vi)

We have
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,0}^{(\beta,m)}\subseteq\left\langle E_{1,1,0}^{(\beta,m)}\right\rangle
. Then, using (v) and applying Proposition
2
, it has order type at most
\omega^{\widehat{a_{1}}}\leq\omega^{\omega a_{1}}
.

(vii)

We define the following sequence :

•

b_{0}=\omega^{\widehat{a_{1}}}

•

b_{k^{\prime}+1}=\omega^{\omega^{\omega(\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+4)b_{k^{\prime}}+1)}}

We show that
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}
is reverse well-ordered with order type less than
b_{k^{\prime}}
. We also claim that the equivalence classes of
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}/{\asymp}
are finite and that

\NR\left(\underset{t\in\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp t\right)\leq\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+4)b_{k^{\prime}}

We show it by induction on
k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
.

•

For
k^{\prime}=0
, we just apply (vi).

•

Assume the property for some
k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
t\in\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}+1}^{(\beta,m)}
. Let
(P_{0},0)(P_{1},i_{1}),\dots,(P_{k+k^{\prime}+1},i_{k+k^{\prime}+1})
minimal for the order
(<_{lex},<)_{lex}
such that
t=e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{0};P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}+1}\end{matrix}\right)
. Then,

t

\ =\

e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{0};P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)-\underset{\ell=m+2}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln_{\ell}{\kappa_{-\beta}}

\qquad-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma>\beta,\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}(k_{P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}+\underset{i=i_{k+1}}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{k+1}(i)|

Write
s=e^{(\beta,m)}\left(\begin{matrix}P_{0};P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\\
P_{k+1},\dots,P_{k+k^{\prime}}\\
i_{1},\dots,i_{k+k^{\prime}}\end{matrix}\right)
. We then have,

\partial(\ln_{m+1}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp t

\ =\

\exp(s)\exp\left(\underset{i=i_{k+1}}{\overset{{\mbox{\raisebox{0.56905pt}{\tiny{$+$}}}\infty}}{\sum}}\ln|P_{k+1}(i)|\right.

\left.\qquad-\underset{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\\
\gamma\ |\ \kappa_{-\gamma}\succeq^{K}P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}(k_{P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}})\end{array}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\hskip-30.00005pt\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}\right)

Consider the following path:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}R(0)=\exp s\\
R(i)=P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}(i-1+i_{k+1})&i>0\end{array}\right.

It is indeed a path since, by definition of
E_{1,k,k^{\prime}+1}^{(\beta,m)}
,
\supp P_{k+k^{\prime}+1}(i_{k+k^{\prime}+1})
must be contained in
\supp s
. Then,

\partial(\ln_{m+1}\kappa_{-\beta})\exp t=\partial R

Moreover,
R\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}\left(\underset{s\in\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp s\right)
. By induction hypothesis and Proposition
1
,
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}+1}^{(\beta,m)}
has order type less than

\omega^{\omega^{\omega(\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+4)b_{k^{\prime}}+1)}}=b_{k^{\prime}+1}

Since the equivalences classes of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}\left(\underset{s\in\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp s\right)/{\asymp}
are finite, the
ones of
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}+1}^{(\beta,m)}/{\asymp}
are also finite. Finally, using Lemmas
6.2.2
and
6.2.2
,

\NR(t)

\ \leq\

(\omega\oplus\omega\otimes\xi\oplus\omega)+\underset{j=0}{\overset{k+1}{\sum}}\underset{i=i_{j}}{\overset{k_{P_{j}}-1}{\sum}}\NR\left(\ln|P_{j}(i)|\right)+\underset{j=0}{\overset{k+1}{\sum}}\max(0,k_{P_{j}}-i_{j})

\ \leq\

\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+4)

Then,
\NR\left(\underset{t^{\prime}\in\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,k^{\prime}+1}^{(\beta,m)}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\exp t^{\prime}\right)\leq\omega(\NR(x)+\xi+4)b_{k^{\prime}+1}

We conclude thanks to the induction principle.

(viii)

By easy induction, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
b_{k^{\prime}}<\lambda
.

(ix)

Using (iv), we get that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\underset{k^{\prime}=0}{\overset{N}{\bigcup}}\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,,k,2k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}
is an initial segment of

\underset{k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,2k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}
. We also have that
\underset{k^{\prime}=0}{\overset{N}{\bigcup}}\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,2k^{\prime}+1}^{(\beta,m)}
is an initial segment of

\underset{k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,2k^{\prime}+1}^{(\beta,m)}
. Using (vii), we get that
\underset{k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,2k^{\prime}}^{(\beta,m)}
has order type at most

\sup\left\{\left.\underset{k=0}{\overset{N}{\oplus}}b_{2k^{\prime}}\ \vphantom{N\in\mathbb{N}}\right|\ N\in\mathbb{N}\right\}=\sup\left\{\left.b_{2N}\ \vphantom{N\in\mathbb{N}}\right|\ N\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\underset{\text{by (viii)}}{\leq}\lambda

Similarly,
\underset{k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,2k^{\prime}+1}^{(\beta,m)}
has order type at most
\lambda
. Using Proposition
2
, we conclude that
E_{1}^{(\beta,m)}
has order type at most
2\lambda
.

Now we deal with the case
(\beta,m)=(\alpha,n)
. A close looking at point (v) above reveals that the property it shows does not depend on
(\beta,m)
. Then we have, using a similar argument as in points (viii) and (ix), that
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}E_{1,k,0}^{(\alpha,n)}
has order type at most
2\lambda
. Then, for any
(\beta,m)\leq_{lex}(\alpha,n)
,
E_{1}^{(\beta,m)}
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
2\lambda
. Using again Proposition
2
and the properties of
E_{2}^{(\beta,m)}
and
E_{3}^{(\beta,m)}
mentioned in the beginning of this proof, we get that
E^{(\beta,m)}
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
2\lambda+\omega(\xi+1)
.
∎

7.2
Length of the series of the anti-derivative of an arbitrary surreal number

See
1

Proof.

Let
\alpha<\gamma
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Write
x=\underset{a\in\supp x}{\overset{}{\sum}}r_{a}\omega^{a}
. For any ordinal
\alpha<\gamma
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
r\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1\}
and any term
s\omega^{a_{0}}
, define
S_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}}
to be the set

\left\{\left.a\in\supp x\ \vphantom{\exists\varepsilon\in\textnormal{No}_{\infty}\ \forall(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}\prec\varepsilon\\
\varepsilon\sim s\omega^{a_{0}}\\
\varepsilon\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}\\
\omega^{a}\asymp\partial(\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha})\exp\varepsilon\end{array}\right.}\right|\ \exists\varepsilon\in\textnormal{No}_{\infty}\ \forall(\beta,m)<_{lex}(\alpha,n)\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\quad}l@{}}\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}\prec\varepsilon\\
\varepsilon\sim s\omega^{a_{0}}\\
\varepsilon\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}\\
\omega^{a}\asymp\partial(\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha})\exp\varepsilon\end{array}\right.\right\}

and consider also

S_{\alpha,n,2,r}=\left\{\left.a\in\supp x\ \vphantom{\exists\varepsilon\in\textnormal{No}_{\infty}\quad\varepsilon\sim r\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}\wedge\omega^{a}\asymp\partial(\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha})\exp\varepsilon}\right|\ \exists\varepsilon\in\textnormal{No}_{\infty}\quad\varepsilon\sim r\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}\wedge\omega^{a}\asymp\partial(\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha})\exp\varepsilon\right\}

x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}}=\underset{a\in S_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}}}{\overset{}{\sum}}r_{a}\omega^{a}\qquad\text{and}\qquad x_{\alpha,n,2,r}=\underset{a\in S_{\alpha,n,2,r}}{\overset{}{\sum}}r_{a}\omega^{a}

All theses surreal numbers have disjoint supports and

x=\underset{s\omega^{a_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}\omega^{\textnormal{No}}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{\alpha<\gamma}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\sum}}x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}}+\underset{r\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1\}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{\alpha<\gamma}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\sum}}x_{\alpha,n,2,r}

We then study both sums of the above equality.

•

The set
\left\{\left.r\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1\}\ \vphantom{S_{\alpha,n,2,r}\neq\varnothing}\right|\ S_{\alpha,n,2,r}\neq\varnothing\right\}
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
\nu(x)
. Let

S_{0}=\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{i}\left(\underset{r\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1\}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{\alpha<\gamma}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\sum}}x_{\alpha,n,2,r}\right)

Since
\Phi
is strongly linear,

S_{0}\subseteq\underset{r\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1\}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{\alpha<\gamma}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{i}(x_{\alpha,n,2,r})

Using Corollary
7.1
,
\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{i}(x_{\alpha,n,2,r})
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
\omega^{\omega(2\lambda+\omega(\gamma+1)+1)}
. Moreover, Lemma
7.1.1
ensure that is
(\alpha,n,r)>_{lex}(\alpha^{\prime},n^{\prime},r^{\prime})
, then
\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{i}(x_{\alpha,n,2,r})<\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{i}(x_{\alpha^{\prime},n^{\prime},2,r^{\prime}})
. We end up with the fact that
\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{i}\left(\underset{r\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1\}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{\alpha<\gamma}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\sum}}x_{\alpha,n,2,r}\right)
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
\omega^{\omega(2\lambda+\omega(\gamma+1)+1)}\nu(x)\gamma
.

•

Let
S_{1}=\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{i}\left(\underset{s\omega^{a_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}\omega^{\textnormal{No}}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{\alpha<\gamma}{\overset{}{\sum}}\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\sum}}x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}}\right)

Since
\Phi
is strongly linear,

S_{1}\subseteq\underset{s\omega^{a_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}\omega^{\textnormal{No}}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{\alpha<\gamma}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{i}(x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}})

Denote
H^{(\beta,m)}(x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}})
,
E^{(\beta,m)}(x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}})
the sets defined as in Corollary
7.1.2
for
x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}}
. Then, using this corollary,
S_{1}
is contained in the set

\underset{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\beta<\gamma\\
m\in\mathbb{N}\\
s\omega^{a_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}\omega^{\textnormal{No}}\end{array}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\alpha,n\ |\ (\beta,m)\leq_{lex}(\alpha,n)\\
\alpha<\gamma\end{array}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in H^{(\beta,m)}(x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}})\\
y\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}(x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}})\right\rangle\end{array}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\hskip-30.00005pt\supp\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}\exp(\eta+y)\right)

We also know that
(\alpha,n,s\omega^{a_{0}})>_{lex}(\alpha^{\prime},n^{\prime},s^{\prime}\omega^{a_{0}^{\prime}})
, then the set

\underset{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in H^{(\beta,m)}(x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}})\\
y\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}(x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}})\right\rangle\end{array}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\hskip-30.00005pt\supp\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}\exp(\eta+y)\right)

is contained in the set

\underset{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in H^{(\beta,m)}\left(x_{\alpha^{\prime},n^{\prime},1,s^{\prime}\omega^{a_{0}^{\prime}}}\right)\\
y\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}\left(x_{\alpha^{\prime},n^{\prime},1,s^{\prime}\omega^{a_{0}^{\prime}}}\right)\right\rangle\end{array}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\hskip-30.00005pt\supp\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}\exp(\eta+y)\right)

Propositions
7.5
and
2
guarantee that all of theses sets are reverse well-ordered with order type less than
\omega^{2\omega\lambda}
. Let

S_{\beta,m}=\underset{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\alpha,n\ |\ (\beta,m)\leq_{lex}(\alpha,n)\\
\alpha<\gamma\\
s\omega^{a_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}\omega^{\textnormal{No}}\end{array}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\underset{\tiny\begin{array}[]{c}\eta\in H^{(\beta,m)}(x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}})\\
y\in\left\langle E^{(\beta,m)}(x_{\alpha,n,1,s\omega^{a_{0}}})\right\rangle\end{array}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\hskip-30.00005pt\supp\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\dfrac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}{\frac{\partial\ln_{m}\kappa_{-\beta}}{\partial\ln_{n}\kappa_{-\alpha}}}\exp(\eta+y)\right)

The set of possible
s\omega^{a_{0}}
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
\nu(x)
. Moreover,
\alpha
and
n
are determined from
s\omega^{a_{0}}
. Then
S_{\beta,m}
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
\omega^{2\omega^{\lambda+1}}\nu(x)
. Finally, if
(\beta,m)>_{lex}(\beta^{\prime},m^{\prime})
, then
S_{\beta,m}\subsetneq S_{\beta^{\prime},m^{\prime}}
and there are at most
\omega\gamma
such couples. Then,
S_{1}
is reverse well-ordered with order type at most
\omega^{2\omega\lambda+1}\nu(x)\gamma
.

Both sets have order type less than
\omega^{\omega^{\lambda+2}}
, which is a multiplicative ordinal. Using Proposition
2
,
\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\supp\Phi^{i}(x)
has order type less than
\omega^{\omega^{\lambda+2}}
.

∎

7.3
Stability of some surreal fields

We are ready to exhibit a surreal field that is stable under
\exp,\ln,\partial
and anti-derivation and that is not
No
itself. We actually have a lot of such fields.

To get a field stable under derivation and anti-derivation, it sufficient that the field is stable under
\exp
and
\ln
, and, if for all
\beta<\alpha
,
\kappa_{-\beta}
is in the field, and if
\omega\otimes\alpha
is less than the authorized length of a serie, then
\kappa_{-\alpha}
must also be in the field.

See
1

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{K}=\underset{\beta<\alpha}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}^{\Gamma_{\beta}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta}}}

As an increasing union of fields,
\mathbb{K}
is indeed a field.

(i)

Using Theorem
1.2
, each field
\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}^{\Gamma_{\beta}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta}}}
is stable under
\exp
and
\ln
, then so is
\mathbb{K}
.

(ii)

Write
\Gamma_{\beta}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta}}=\left(\Gamma_{i,\beta}\right)_{i<\gamma_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}}
. We use the notation introduce in the beginning Definition
1
. We prove by induction on
i<\gamma_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}
that for
x\in\Gamma_{i,\beta}
,
\NR(\omega^{x})<\eta_{\beta}e_{i}
.

•

For
i=0
we have
e_{0}=1
and
\Gamma_{0,\beta}=\Gamma_{\beta}
. By assumption on
\Gamma_{\beta}
, for all
x\in\Gamma_{0,\beta}
,
\NR(\omega^{x})<\eta_{\beta}=\eta_{\beta}e_{0}
.

•

Assume the property for some ordinal
i
. Then let
x\in\Gamma_{i+1,\beta}
. Write
x=u+v+\underset{k=1}{\overset{p}{\sum}}h(w_{k})
with
u\in\Gamma_{i,\beta}
,
v\in\mathbb{R}_{e_{i}}^{g\left((\Gamma_{i,\beta})_{+}^{*}\right)}
and
w_{k}
such
r\omega^{w_{k}}
is a term of some element
y_{k}\in\Gamma_{i,\beta}
, for some
r\in\mathbb{R}
. Using Corollary
6.2.2
,

\NR(\omega^{x})\leq\NR(\omega^{u})+\NR(\omega^{v})+\underset{k=1}{\overset{p}{\sum}}\NR(\omega^{h(w_{k})})+p+1

From induction hypothesis,

\NR(\omega^{u})<\eta_{\beta}e_{i}

Write
v=\underset{j<\nu}{\overset{}{\sum}}r_{j}\omega^{g(a_{j})}
. Then
\omega^{v}=\exp\left(\underset{j<\nu}{\overset{}{\sum}}r_{j}\omega^{a_{j}}\right)
. From induction hypothesis,
\NR(\omega^{a_{j}})<\eta_{\beta}e_{i}
. Then
\NR(r_{j}\omega^{a_{j}})<\eta_{\beta}e_{i}+1
. Then

\NR(\omega^{v})=\NR\left(\underset{j<\nu}{\overset{}{\sum}}r_{j}\omega^{a_{j}}\right)\leq(\eta_{\beta}e_{i}+1)\otimes\nu\leq(\eta_{\beta}e_{i}+1)\otimes e_{i}\leq\eta_{\beta}e_{i}^{2}

We also have

\NR(\omega^{h(w_{k})})=\NR(\omega^{\omega^{w_{k}}})\leq\NR(\omega^{y_{k}})<\eta_{\beta}e_{i}

Finally,
\NR(\omega^{x})\leq(p+1)(\eta_{\beta}e_{i}+1)+\eta_{\beta}e_{i}^{2}<\eta_{\beta}e_{i+1}

•

Assume
i
is a limit ordinal. Then by definition of
\Gamma_{i,\beta}
for any
x\in\Gamma_{i,\beta}
there is some
j<i
such that
x\in\Gamma_{j,\beta}
. Then induction hypothesis concludes.

(iii)

Let
x\in\mathbb{K}
and
\beta<\alpha
such that
x\in\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}^{\Gamma_{\beta}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta}}}
. Using (ii), there is
i<\gamma_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}
such that

\NR(x)\leq(\eta_{\beta}e_{i}+1)\otimes\nu(x)<\eta_{\beta}\otimes\varepsilon_{\beta}=\varepsilon_{\beta}

Since
\eta_{\beta}\otimes\varepsilon_{\beta}
is a limit ordinal, then we also have
\NR(x)+1<\eta_{\beta}\otimes\varepsilon_{\beta}=\varepsilon_{\beta}
.

(iv)

Let
x\in\mathbb{K}
and
\beta<\alpha
such that
x\in\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}^{\Gamma_{\beta}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta}}}
. Using (iii) and Proposition
1
,
\nu(\partial x)<\omega^{\omega^{\omega(\NR(x)+1)}}<\varepsilon_{\beta}
. Using Corollary
1
and (i), we also have for all
P\in\mathcal{P}(x)
,
\partial P\in\mathbb{R}\omega^{\Gamma_{\beta}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta}}}
. Then,

\partial x\in\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}^{\Gamma_{\beta}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta}}}\subseteq\mathbb{K}

Then
\mathbb{K}
is stable under
\partial
.

(v)

Let
x\in\mathbb{K}
and
\beta<\alpha
such that
x\in\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}^{\Gamma_{\beta}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta}}}
. Using Proposition
1
and the definition of
\mathcal{A}
,

\nu\left(\mathcal{A}\circ\left(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\Phi^{i}\right)(x)\right)<\omega^{\omega^{\lambda+2}}

where
\lambda
is least
\varepsilon
-number greater
\NR(x)
and such that

\forall P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(x)\qquad\kappa_{-\lambda}\prec^{K}P(k_{P})

Using (iii),
\NR(x)<\varepsilon_{\beta}
. Let
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{L}}(x)
. Using (i),
\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{\beta}}^{\Gamma_{\beta}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta}}}
is stable under
\exp
and
ln
. Since
P(i+1)
is a term of
\ln|P(i)|
, if
P(i)\in\omega^{\Gamma_{\beta}}
, then
P(i+1)\in\omega^{\Gamma_{\beta}}
. By induction,
P(k_{P})\in\omega^{\Gamma_{\beta}}
. Since
P(k_{P})
is infinitely large,
P(k_{P})\in\omega^{\left(\Gamma_{\beta}\right)^{*}_{+}}
. By assumption on
\Gamma_{\beta}
,
P(k_{P})\succ^{K}\kappa_{-\varepsilon_{\beta}}
. Finally,
\lambda\leq\varepsilon_{\beta}
and

\nu\left(\mathcal{A}\circ\left(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\Phi^{i}\right)(x)\right)<\omega^{\omega^{\varepsilon_{\beta}+2}}<\varepsilon_{\beta+1}

Propositions
7.1.1
and
7.4
and the third assumption about
\Gamma_{\beta}
ensure that each term of
\mathcal{A}\circ\left(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\Phi^{i}\right)(x)
is in
\omega^{\Gamma_{\beta}}\subseteq\omega^{\Gamma_{\beta+1}}
. Then

\mathcal{A}\circ\left(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\sum}}\Phi^{i}\right)(x)\in\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{\beta+1}}^{\Gamma_{\beta+1}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{\beta+1}}}

Application of Corollary
6.5
gives that
\mathbb{K}
is stable under anti-derivation.

∎

The previous theorem may seem have a lot of strong hypothesis but we can actually give a non-trivial application.

Take
\alpha=\omega
and for
n<\omega
,
\Gamma_{n}=\left\{\left.x\in\textnormal{No}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\ \vphantom{\NR(\omega^{x})<\varepsilon_{n-1}}\right|\ \NR(\omega^{x})<\varepsilon_{n-1}\right\}
, with
\varepsilon_{-1}:=\omega
.
We first recall that from Lemma
6.1
, for any ordinal
\alpha
,

\kappa_{-\alpha}=\omega^{\omega^{-\omega\otimes\alpha}}

in particular
\kappa_{-\varepsilon_{n}}=\omega^{\omega^{-\omega\otimes\varepsilon_{n}}}=\omega^{\omega^{-\varepsilon_{n}}}=\omega^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}}}

From Theorem
3.7
, we know that the sign sequence of
\omega^{-\omega\otimes\alpha}
is
(+)(-)^{\omega\otimes\alpha}
, which has length
1\oplus\omega\otimes\alpha
.

•

Since
\varepsilon_{n}
is an
\varepsilon
-number, hence an additive ordinal, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\Gamma_{n}
is an abelian group.

•

Of course for any
n\leq m
,
\Gamma_{n}\subseteq\Gamma_{m}
.

•

Since
\left|\omega^{-\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{+-}=1\oplus\omega\otimes\varepsilon_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}
, we have
\kappa_{-\varepsilon_{n}}\notin\omega^{\Gamma_{\beta}}
. However, for
\alpha<\varepsilon_{n}
,
\left|\omega^{-\omega\otimes\alpha}\right|_{+-}=1\oplus\omega\otimes\alpha<\varepsilon_{n}
, and, since
\kappa_{-\alpha}\in\mathbb{L}
,
\NR(\kappa_{-\alpha})=0<\varepsilon_{n-1}
. Therefor, we have
\kappa_{-\alpha}\in\omega^{\Gamma_{\beta}}
. Since,
\kappa_{-\varepsilon_{\beta}}\in\omega^{\textnormal{No}}
, is
x\in\omega^{\Gamma_{\beta}}
is such that
x\preceq^{K}\kappa_{-\varepsilon_{\beta}}
then
\kappa_{-\varepsilon_{\beta}}
is a prefix of
x
and
\left|x\right|_{+-}\geq\varepsilon_{\beta}
what is impossible from Lemma
5.4
.

•

We can take
\eta_{\beta}=\varepsilon_{\beta-1}<\varepsilon_{\eta}
.

Theorem
1
applies and
\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\Gamma_{n}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{n}}}
is stable under
\exp
,
\ln
,
\partial
and anti-derivation. As a final note, we can notice that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\Gamma_{n}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{n}}}=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\bigcup}}\mathbb{R}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\textnormal{No}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\uparrow\varepsilon_{n}}}
